The calculation using the 1985 national wage remains uncorrected because the objection was waived, not because it is the lawful general wage rule for scheduled hearing loss. The 82.4 percent figure was not challenged, and the Court awards no new amount or remand recalculation. Retirement audiograms may identify the compensable loss; the Court imposes no mandatory testing duty and decides no new aging-apportionment dispute.
- **Commissioner v. Soliman, 1993-01-12:** Supreme Court merits review is complete. The Fourth Circuit judgment is reversed and Soliman’s claimed deduction under §280A(c)(1)(A) is disallowed on this record. The Court gives no new deduction amount, penalty ruling or tax computation, and creates no retained proceeding or new deadline. The unappealed travel-deduction determinations are untouched.
- **Rowland v. California Men’s Colony, Unit II Men’s Advisory Council, 1993-01-12:** Supreme Court review of the statutory eligibility question is complete. The Ninth Circuit’s rejection of categorical association ineligibility is affirmed; its direction that the district court automatically grant the motion is vacated. The matter returns through the Ninth Circuit to the district court for the authorized affidavit, actual financial-resource showing, lawful representation and applicable statutory screening. No automatic status, compulsory counsel appointment, merits relief, new damages remedy or predetermined finding of indigence is ordered.
- **Nixon v. United States, 1993-01-13:** Supreme Court review complete; dismissal stands; no reinstatement, salary award or supervisory remand.
- **Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, 1993-01-13:** State-foundation remand; no automatic injunction dissolution or hindrance merits judgment.
- **Crosby v. United States, 1993-01-13:** Supreme Court merits review is complete. The Court neither fixes a new trial date nor enters a separate flight sanction or a revised restitution award. No retained Supreme Court proceeding or nonroutine follow-on act is established.
- **Dobbs v. Zant, 1993-01-19:** Supreme Court review complete; Eleventh Circuit remand; ineffectiveness merits unresolved.
- **Herrera v. Collins, 1993-01-25:** Supreme Court review complete; appellate vacation of federal stay remains; no new hearing/writ/stay or execution date.

- **Zafiro v. United States, 1993-01-25:** Supreme Court review is complete; the Seventh Circuit judgment stands. No petitioner receives a new trial on the severance ground, and no remand revives Gloria Zafiro’s omitted claim. No retained proceeding or later collateral disposition is established.

Graham materially changes the legal premise for the later Johnson v. Texas event; refresh that packet before adjudication. Later innocence applications must preserve Herrera’s unresolved general claim and burden. The January25 group is now complete. Growe’s three controlling propositions and its dismissal direction entered Voinovich only after durable preservation and affected-packet refresh.

A Parcel of Land Known as 92 Buena Vista Avenue, 1993-02-24:** The Court affirms the defense-eligibility ruling and remands for further proceedings consistent with the two statutory holdings. - **McNeil v. United States, 1993-05-17:** The Seventh Circuit judgment dismissing the original premature FTCA action is affirmed. No tort liability is adjudicated. On remand, determine member residence in Indian country and apply the income-source and vehicle-tax rules to the established facts. No blanket exemption for all members, relief for nonmembers, or tax refund amount is adjudicated.
- **Deal v. United States, 1993-05-17:** The reviewed sentence is affirmed: five years on the first firearm conviction and twenty years on each of five additional firearm convictions, all consecutive, for 105 years under §924(c). No conviction, other sentencing component or constitutional proportionality issue is adjudicated. The dissenting proposed base terms would total thirty years under §924(c), consecutively, but that is not the mandate.
- **El Vocero de Puerto Rico v. Puerto Rico, 1993-05-17:** The judgment sustaining blanket closure is reversed and the matter remanded for proceedings consistent with qualified public access. The lower courts may protect an established overriding interest through specific findings, consideration of reasonable alternatives and a narrowly confined order. No automatic release of every transcript, grand-jury opening, damages award or special deadline is directed.
- **Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc., 1993-05-24:** The judgment excluding the transfers from §4975(c)(1)(A) is reversed and the case expressly remanded for proceedings under the correct classification and applicable tax conditions. No new valuation, final tax computation, unpresented exemption or automatic second-tier assessment is adjudicated; statutory correction and exemption conditions continue to govern if properly applicable.
- **United States Department of Justice v. Landano, 1993-05-24:** The reviewed judgment is vacated and confidentiality determinations remanded under the express-assurance or supported-inference standard. The agency retains its burden; the requester may rebut an inference using nonprotected evidence. In-camera affidavits or other protective procedures may be used when justified, not required in every case. Reasonably segregable nonexempt material must be released, but deleting a name does not alone require release of protected information furnished by a confidential criminal-investigation source. No blanket disclosure, retrial or innocence finding is ordered.
- **Lincoln v. Vigil, 1993-05-24:** The judgment is reversed and remanded. The lower court may not compel this allocation on the rejected APA grounds. The distinct due-process claim remains unresolved; the Court does not decide the §552 publication question, the independently unreviewed remedial question, or an entitlement to a particular hearing. The decision supplies no authority to spend outside the enacted mission or disregard an independently enforceable legal restriction.

- **Sullivan v. Louisiana, 1993-06-01:** The Louisiana judgment sustaining the conviction is reversed and remanded for proceedings permitting a new trial under a constitutionally valid reasonable-doubt charge. No acquittal or sufficiency ruling is entered. The prior penalty-phase relief is undisturbed; the original death sentence is not reinstated. Any sentence after a new conviction must follow the lawful proceedings then required. No retrial date or remedy for unrelated convictions is set.
- **Smith v. United States, 1993-06-01:** The firearm-use conviction and resulting thirty-year consecutive term are affirmed. The Court does not adjudicate the separate drug convictions or an uncharged carrying theory. No completed exchange is required on the trafficking offenses and offer proved here. The antecedent availability of a §502(a)(3) action against this nonfiduciary is reserved, not rejected as a jurisdictional defect.
